# SSX 2026 CSI Division 46 — Water and Wastewater Equipment Activity List

This master library lists potential schedule activities for Division 46 water and wastewater process equipment. Select only activities applicable to the project, split work by treatment train, process unit, equipment tag, basin, building, area, elevation, and outage window, and verify section numbers and titles against the project specifications and the licensed/current CSI MasterFormat source.

## Scheduling controls

- Treat process definition, permitting, design reviews, equipment procurement, factory acceptance testing, delivery, installation, clean-water testing, process startup, biological stabilization, performance testing, operator training, and turnover as separate activities when they affect the critical path.
- Include live-plant interfaces, regulatory discharge requirements, temporary treatment, bypass pumping, storage or hauling, shutdowns, drain-down, flushing, disinfection, dechlorination, decontamination, odor control, and return-to-service constraints.
- Coordinate chemical safety, spill containment, ventilation, gas detection, emergency treatment, hazardous locations, lockout/tagout, confined-space entry, fall protection, biosafety, and environmental permits.
- Plan required sampling, chain of custody, certified laboratory analysis, permit compliance, OEM attendance, seeding and acclimation, seasonal or deferred testing, and owner operations staffing.
- Record design and peak flow, hydraulic profile, process loading, turndown, redundancy, influent and effluent quality, solids or chemical characteristics, performance guarantees, energy and chemical use, and acceptance criteria for each equipment package.
- Break installation and commissioning down by treatment train, process unit, basin, skid, equipment tag, electrical or controls boundary, and approved outage window rather than using one project-wide activity.

## 46 01 00 — Operation and Maintenance of Water and Wastewater Equipment

- Review record drawings, OEM manuals, operating history, alarms, maintenance logs, water-quality data, and prior inspections for 46 01 00 Operation and Maintenance of Water and Wastewater Equipment
- Perform condition assessment and document corrosion, wear, fouling, leakage, vibration, alignment, coating condition, instrumentation, controls, and safety-system status
- Define process outage, temporary treatment or bypass, storage, hauling, permit constraints, isolation points, and return-to-service milestones
- Prepare maintenance procedures, repair submittals, environmental and safety permits, OEM support plan, spare-parts plan, and quality-control hold points
- Conduct pre-outage coordination with operations, maintenance, laboratory, safety, environmental, controls, electrical, and affected process trades
- Shut down, drain, flush, purge, decontaminate, isolate, lock out, and verify zero-energy and safe-entry conditions
- Disassemble, clean, inspect, measure, and test accessible mechanical, process, electrical, pneumatic, hydraulic, instrumentation, and control components
- Repair or replace worn, corroded, fouled, damaged, or obsolete components, coatings, seals, bearings, drives, instruments, cables, and controls
- Reassemble with verified clearances, alignment, torque, lubrication, cleanliness, coating repairs, calibration, guarding, and software configuration
- Perform leak, pressure, rotation, vibration, electrical, calibration, alarm, interlock, emergency-stop, and dry-run testing
- Return equipment to process service and verify capacity, treatment performance, chemical usage, energy demand, effluent quality, and stable operation
- Correct deficiencies, update asset and maintenance records, reconcile spares, and deliver test reports, warranties, backups, and turnover records

## 46 05 00 — Common Work Results for Water and Wastewater Equipment

- Confirm process, influent, effluent, capacity, redundancy, turndown, reliability, water-quality, environmental, and future-expansion criteria for 46 05 00 Common Work Results for Water and Wastewater Equipment
- Field-verify equipment space, access, lifting paths, maintenance clearances, tanks, channels, foundations, elevations, hydraulic profile, and tie-in conditions
- Coordinate process, civil, structural, architectural, mechanical, electrical, controls, fire-protection, chemical, laboratory, safety, and utility interfaces
- Complete delegated engineering, hydraulic and structural calculations, code and hazard reviews, environmental permits, and authority approvals
- Prepare and obtain approval of product data, certified layouts, calculations, materials, coatings, P&IDs, control narratives, test plans, and OEM qualifications
- Release long-lead equipment, drives, instruments, analyzers, controls, specialty materials, media, membranes, lamps, and accessories
- Complete design reviews, shop inspections, material traceability, weld and coating checks, pressure tests, drive runs, software tests, and factory acceptance testing
- Verify foundations, anchors, embeds, supports, channels, tanks, access, drainage, utility rough-ins, and predecessor work are ready
- Receive, inspect, preserve, and stage equipment; protect machined surfaces, coatings, membranes, media, electronics, instruments, and loose components
- Execute the approved rigging plan and set, level, align, anchor, grout, assemble, coat, identify, and protect equipment
- Connect process piping, drains, vents, air, gas, chemicals, washwater, power, grounding, controls, networks, sampling, and safety systems
- Complete inspections, startup, capacity and treatment testing, deficiency correction, training, spares, records, warranties, and turnover

## 46 05 05 — Selective Demolition for Water and Wastewater Equipment

- Survey existing process equipment, structures, coatings, hazardous materials, utilities, instrumentation, controls, and demolition limits for 46 05 05 Selective Demolition for Water and Wastewater Equipment
- Confirm treatment outage windows, temporary treatment or bypass, flow diversion, storage or hauling, owner salvage, decontamination, and waste-disposal requirements
- Prepare demolition, engineered-lift, process-isolation, spill-control, confined-space, environmental, infection-control if applicable, and permitting plans
- Sample and characterize residual liquids, chemicals, screenings, grit, sludge, media, scale, coatings, and other regulated or hazardous materials
- Identify, label, and protect equipment, piping, channels, tanks, structures, electrical systems, controls, and process units to remain
- Back up programs, configurations, trends, calibration data, and operating records before shutdown
- Drain, flush, neutralize, purge, decontaminate, isolate, lock out, and verify zero-energy and safe-atmosphere conditions
- Disconnect process, chemical, air, gas, water, drain, power, grounding, network, and control connections at approved boundaries
- Dismantle and remove equipment, supports, platforms, piping, wiring, instruments, coatings, and accessories in the approved sequence
- Rig, transport, preserve, salvage, recycle, or dispose of equipment and residuals with manifests and chain-of-custody records
- Cap, blind, seal, label, and test retained connections; patch penetrations, restore coatings and containment, and verify remaining systems operate
- Complete deficiency correction, cleaning, salvage reconciliation, waste records, permit closeout, and demolition turnover documentation

## 46 06 00 — Schedules for Water and Wastewater Equipment

- Compile the equipment, asset-tag, and process-system register for 46 06 00 Schedules for Water and Wastewater Equipment
- Record service, process stream, design and peak flow, loading, capacity, redundancy, turndown, and criticality
- Record manufacturer, model, size, materials, coatings, weight, center of gravity, drive, speed, power, and hazardous-area rating
- Record influent and effluent criteria, solids or chemical characteristics, operating ranges, performance guarantees, and permit limits
- Record foundations, anchors, access, lifting provisions, maintenance clearances, environmental conditions, and installation location
- Record piping, valves, drains, vents, sampling, washwater, air, gas, chemical, electrical, grounding, network, and controls interfaces
- Record instruments, analyzers, alarms, permissives, interlocks, emergency stops, fail positions, and control-panel references
- Record submittal, delegated-design, permit, fabrication, factory-test, delivery, installation, and OEM-support milestones
- Record preservation, lubrication, consumables, media, membranes, lamps, reagents, spare parts, and special-tool requirements
- Record inspections, pressure or leak tests, calibration, mechanical-completion, startup, performance-test, and laboratory-sampling requirements
- Coordinate schedule data with process flow diagrams, P&IDs, equipment lists, I/O lists, electrical loads, commissioning plan, and asset database
- Issue the approved schedule, resolve discrepancies, and incorporate final nameplate, setpoint, test, warranty, and as-built data

## 46 07 00 — Packaged Water and Wastewater Treatment Equipment

- Field-verify equipment space, hydraulic elevations, foundations, access, lifting paths, maintenance clearances, utilities, drainage, containment, and tie-in conditions

## Library controls

- Project specifications, approved process design, permits, authority requirements, and manufacturer instructions govern final activity selection and acceptance criteria.
- Each selected activity must carry the Division 46 section, process area, treatment train, equipment tag or system boundary, responsible party, predecessor, successor, planned duration, calendar, and required inspection or test.
- Procurement, fabrication, factory testing, delivery, installation, inspection, startup, performance testing, correction, training, and turnover remain separate activities when meaningful.
- Live-plant shutdowns, temporary treatment, chemical introductions, biological seeding, permit sampling, and return to service require explicit owner authorization milestones.
- Reserved or unused sections are not invented. Project-specific numbering is versioned and cross-referenced without silently overwriting the master library.

## Research basis

- CSI MasterFormat is the governing organizational standard; the project’s licensed/current edition and specifications control.
- Public guide specifications, owner standards, regulatory requirements, and manufacturer procedures may validate construction and commissioning scope but do not replace project requirements.
